1	# Chores-03
2	
3	## Add `fn claude-symlink`
4	
5	Port claude-symlink from to shell script below to a fn so
6	it can be used with a "new" command which will create the dual repos.
7	The base fn should probably not do any I/O it should return
8	approprate error information and other `fn` so the caller can
9	handle errors as needed.
10	```
11	#!/bin/bash
12	# claude-symlink.sh - Create a symlink for Claude Code project directories
13	# Authors: wink@saville.com and Claude Opus 4.5
14	
15	set -euo pipefail
16	
17	
18	usage() {
19	    echo "Usage: $(basename "$0") [-l] <target> [symlink-dir]" >&2
20	    echo "  -l          - List contents of symlinked directory after creation" >&2
21	    echo "  target      - Path to link to (e.g., ./.claude)" >&2
22	    echo "  symlink-dir - Directory for symlink (default: \$HOME/.claude/projects)" >&2
23	    exit 1
24	}
25	
26	list_contents=false
27	
28	while getopts ":l" opt; do
29	    case $opt in
30	        l) list_contents=true ;;
31	        *) usage ;;
32	    esac
33	done
34	shift $((OPTIND - 1))
35	
36	if [[ $# -lt 1 ]]; then
37	    usage
38	fi
39	
40	target="$1"
41	symlink_dir="${2:-$HOME/.claude/projects}"
42	
43	# Create target directory if it doesn't exist
44	if [[ ! -e "$target" ]]; then
45	    mkdir -p "$target"
46	    echo "Created target directory: $target"
47	fi
48	
49	# Resolve target to absolute path
50	abs_target="$(cd "$(dirname "$target")" && pwd)/$(basename "$target")"
51	
52	# Get current directory and convert to dash-separated name
53	# Claude Code encodes paths by replacing / and . with -
54	cwd="$(pwd)"
55	symlink_name="${cwd//\//-}"
56	symlink_name="${symlink_name//./-}"
57	
58	# Ensure symlink directory exists
59	mkdir -p "$symlink_dir"
60	
61	symlink_path="$symlink_dir/$symlink_name"
62	
63	# Check if something already exists at symlink path
64	if [[ -e "$symlink_path" || -L "$symlink_path" ]]; then
65	    if [[ -L "$symlink_path" ]]; then
66	        current_target="$(readlink "$symlink_path")"
67	        echo "Existing symlink: $symlink_path -> $current_target"
68	        read -rp "Replace with new target? [y/N] " response
69	        if [[ ! "$response" =~ ^[Yy]$ ]]; then
70	            echo "Aborted." >&2
71	            exit 1
72	        fi
73	    else
74	        echo "Error: '$symlink_path' exists and is not a symlink" >&2
75	        exit 1
76	    fi
77	fi
78	
79	# Create the symlink
80	ln -sfn "$abs_target" "$symlink_path"
81	
82	echo "Created: $symlink_path -> $abs_target"
83	
84	if [[ "$list_contents" == true ]]; then
85	    echo ""
86	    echo "Contents of $symlink_path:"
87	    ls -la "$symlink_path"/
88	fi
89	```

43	          ok   — ochid trailer is valid\n  \
44	          err  — ochid has issues (wrong prefix, wrong length, ID not found)\n  \
45	          miss — no ochid trailer; shows match from other repo if found"
46	    )]
47	    ValidateDesc(validate_desc::ValidateDescArgs),
48	
49	    /// Fix commit descriptions against the other repo (dry-run by default)
50	    #[command(long_about = "Fix commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
51	        Default is dry-run; use --no-dry-run to write changes.\n\n\
52	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
53	        Status labels:\n  \
54	          ok    — ochid trailer is valid (no change)\n  \
55	          fix   — ochid has issues, shows proposed fix (dry-run)\n  \
56	          fixed — ochid was rewritten (--no-dry-run)\n  \
57	          add   — missing ochid, match found, shows proposed addition (dry-run)\n  \
58	          added — missing ochid was added (--no-dry-run)\n  \
59	          skip  — skipped (no ochid, no match, or max-fixes reached)\n  \
60	          err   — ID not found and no --fallback provided")]
61	    FixDesc(fix_desc::FixDescArgs),
62	
63	    /// Squash working copy into target commit
64	    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
65	}

72	        Commands::Chid(args) => {

78	        }

81	                eprintln!("error: {e}");
82	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
83	            }
84	            ExitCode::SUCCESS
85	        }

87	            if let Err(e) = list::list(&args) {
88	                eprintln!("error: {e}");
89	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
90	            }
91	            ExitCode::SUCCESS
92	        }
93	        Commands::Show(args) => {
94	            if let Err(e) = show::show(&args) {
95	                eprintln!("error: {e}");
96	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
97	            }
98	            ExitCode::SUCCESS
99	        }
100	        Commands::ValidateDesc(args) => {
101	            if let Err(e) = validate_desc::validate_desc(&args) {
102	                eprintln!("error: {e}");
103	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
104	            }
105	            ExitCode::SUCCESS
106	        }
107	        Commands::FixDesc(args) => {
108	            if let Err(e) = fix_desc::fix_desc(&args) {
109	                eprintln!("error: {e}");
110	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
111	            }
112	            ExitCode::SUCCESS
113	        }
114	        Commands::Finalize(args) => {
115	            let opts = args.into_opts();
116	            finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize entry");
117	            match finalize::finalize(&opts) {
118	                Ok(()) => {
119	                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize exit ok");
120	                    ExitCode::SUCCESS
121	                }
122	                Err(e) => {
123	                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("main: finalize exit err={e}"));
124	                    eprintln!("error: {e}");
125	                    ExitCode::FAILURE
126	                }
127	            }
128	        }
129	    }
130	}

# vc-x1 symlink subcommand design spec

## Open questions

- How does claude-code "project-files" work on MacOS and Windows, in parituclar
  will the symlink trick work?
 
## Background

Claude Code stores session data in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/`. We use
a dual-repo scheme where the session data lives in a `.claude` directory that is
a separate jj-git repo (the "session repo"), sibling to the code repo. A symlink
in `~/.claude/projects/` points to this local `.claude` directory so Claude Code
finds its session data there.

We currently use a bash script (`claude-symlink.sh`) for this. This spec ports
that functionality into vc-x1 as a native Rust subcommand, so `new` and `clone`
commands (planned later) can call it programmatically.

## Path encoding convention

The symlink name is the absolute working directory with every `/` replaced by `-`.

Example: cwd `/home/wink/data/prgs/vc-x1` produces symlink name
`-home-wink-data-prgs-vc-x1`, and the full symlink is:

```
~/.claude/projects/-home-wink-data-prgs-vc-x1 -> /home/wink/data/prgs/vc-x1/.claude
```

## CLI interface

```
vc-x1 symlink [OPTIONS] [TARGET]
```

Arguments:
- `TARGET` — directory to link to (default: read `other-repo` from
  `.vc-config.toml`, fallback to `.claude`)

Options:
- `--symlink-dir <PATH>` — override symlink parent directory
  (default: `~/.claude/projects`)
- `-l, --list` — list contents of the symlinked directory after creation
  (use the symlink path with trailing `/` to verify the symlink actually works)
- `-y, --yes` — replace existing symlink without prompting (needed for
  non-interactive use by future `new`/`clone` subcommands)

## Behavior

1. Resolve target to absolute path. If target directory doesn't exist, create it
   with `create_dir_all`.
2. Get `current_dir()`, convert to string, replace all `/` with `-` to form
   the symlink name.
3. Ensure symlink parent directory exists (`create_dir_all`).
4. Check what exists at the symlink path:
   - **Nothing** → create symlink.
   - **Existing symlink** → print current target via `read_link()`, prompt
     "Replace? [y/N]" unless `--yes` was passed. On yes, remove and recreate.
     On no, abort.
   - **File or directory** (not a symlink) → error and abort. Use
     `symlink_metadata()` (doesn't follow symlinks) to distinguish.
5. Create symlink with `std::os::unix::fs::symlink()`.
6. Print: `Created: <symlink_path> -> <abs_target>`
7. If `--list`, print contents by reading through the symlink path (end-to-end
   verification that the symlink works).

## Target default resolution

Fallback chain for target when no argument given:
1. `.vc-config.toml` field `workspace.other-repo` (already used elsewhere in vc-x1)
2. `.claude`

## Implementation notes

- `cfg(unix)` — `libc` is already a dependency. The `std::os::unix::fs::symlink`
  function is available. Windows support is not a current concern.
- Interactive prompt: use `std::io::stdin().read_line()` — no need for an
  external crate.
- This should be implemented as a reusable function (not just a subcommand
```

# vc-x1 new and clone subcommands design spec

## Prerequisites

- The `symlink` subcommand from `symlink-design.md` must be implemented first.
  Both `new` and `clone` call the symlink logic internally (with `yes: true`).
- `gh` CLI must be installed and authenticated (`gh auth status`). Check early,
  error with a clear message if missing.
- `jj` must be installed.

## Conventions

- Code repo: `<name>` (e.g. `my-project`)
- Session repo: `<name>.claude` (always `<name>` + `.claude` suffix)
- Submodule path inside code repo: `.claude`
- GitHub owner: inferred from `gh` (`gh api user --jq .login`), overridable
  with `--owner`
- SSH URLs: `git@github.com:<owner>/<repo>.git`

## `vc-x1 new <name>`

Creates a new dual-repo project from scratch, seeded from the template repos.

### CLI

```
vc-x1 new <name> [OPTIONS]
```

Arguments:
- `NAME` — project name (becomes both the directory name and GitHub repo name)

Options:
- `--owner <OWNER>` — GitHub user/org (default: current `gh` user)
- `--dir <PATH>` — parent directory to create project in (default: cwd)

### Steps

All commands below are described as what the tool executes. Abort on any
failure with a clear message about what step failed.

#### 1. Preflight checks

- Verify `gh` is installed and authenticated
- Verify `jj` is installed
- Verify `<dir>/<name>` does not already exist locally
- Verify neither `<owner>/<name>` nor `<owner>/<name>.claude` exist on GitHub
  (`gh repo view <owner>/<name>` — check exit code)

#### 2. Create the session repo (`<name>.claude`)

This must exist on GitHub before we can `git submodule add` it.

```sh
gh repo create <owner>/<name>.claude --public --clone
cd <name>.claude
```

Seed it from the template's session repo content. For the first pass, the
simplest approach:

```sh
gh repo clone winksaville/vc-template-x1.claude -- --depth 1 /tmp/<tmpdir>
# Copy all files except .git from tmpdir into <name>.claude
# rm -rf /tmp/<tmpdir>
```

Rewrite `.vc-config.toml` to fix the `workspace.path` to `/.claude` (should
already be correct from template, but verify/write it).

Do obvious string substitutions in copied files:
- In `.vc-config.toml`: no change expected (path is `/.claude`, other-repo
  is `..` or `/`)
- In any `CLAUDE.md` or `README.md`: replace `vc-template-x1` with `<name>`

Initial commit and push:

```sh
jj git init --colocate
jj describe -m "feat: Initial session repo for <name>"
```

Got it. I can see the full picture now.

**What you have**: chores-03.md describes three features at a high level — `claude-symlink` as a reusable function (ported from the bash script), `new` to create dual repos from scratch, and `clone` to clone existing dual repos.

**What Claude produced**: Very prescriptive design docs — exact CLI flags, step-by-step implementation sequences, specific function signatures. You deleted those because they were too rigid.

**What chores-03.md keeps**: Just the intent — the bash script to port, the goals of `new` and `clone`, and one useful architectural hint: the symlink function should avoid doing I/O directly and instead return results/errors so callers can handle them.

That's a reasonable approach — the bash script plus the existing codebase patterns (how other subcommands are structured, `common.rs`, `toml_simple.rs`, etc.) give me plenty to work from without a rigid spec. The design decisions around CLI args, error handling, `gh`/`jj` invocation strategy, and template vs fresh-init can be worked out as we go.

Want to start on one of these? If so, `claude-symlink` is the natural first step since `new` and `clone` both depend on it. Should I look at the current version and Cargo.toml to understand the versioning baseline before planning?

On the new command, I'm thinking the initial implementaion should be as simple as possible, probably create the two repos with only vc-config.toml, .gitignore, .jj/ and ./git. In addition, the code repo will contain .gitmodules so `git clone --recursive ..` can be used to clone both projects at once, thoughts?

> AGENT

That's a solid approach — much simpler than the template-based design. A few thoughts:

**Submodule for `.claude`** is the key insight. It means `clone` barely needs to exist as a separate command — it's mostly `git clone --recursive` + `jj git init` in both repos + symlink. The heavy lifting is in `new`.

**Minimal `new` flow** as I understand it:
1. Create two GitHub repos (`<name>` and `<name>.claude`) via `gh`
2. Init both locally with `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`
3. Add `.claude` as a submodule in the code repo (gives you `.gitmodules`)
4. Drop `vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` in each
5. Initial commit + push both
6. Run the symlink logic

**Questions/considerations:**
- The submodule URL needs to be the GitHub SSH/HTTPS URL for `.claude` repo so recursive clone works for others. That means the GitHub repos need to exist before `git submodule add`.
- `.gitignore` — presumably `target/` in the code repo, and maybe session-specific stuff in `.claude`? Or keep both minimal and let the user fill them in?
- Does `vc-config.toml` need anything beyond `workspace.other-repo` and `workspace.path` at this point?
- Order of operations: create both GitHub repos first, then do all local work? That way if GitHub creation fails for the second repo, you haven't left a half-built state.

The symlink function is still a prerequisite for step 6, so the implementation order would be: symlink fn first, then `new`. Does that match your thinking, or do you want to tackle them together?

> DEVELOPER

1) In my experimentation it does appear the <name>.claude repo must exist before executing the `git submodule add`, the <name> repo does not. Also, to create the ochid the both repos must be created locally before commiting. Those could be `squash`d in that doesn't seem necessary. So creating both I'm thinking creating both in place and fully "functional" and the last steps would be to push <name>.claude then `git submodule add` and finally push <name>. That means the "Initial Commit" of <name> has no user code. I don't see that as a big problem, the user can squash and change description if desired or just the user code becomes the second commit. Thoughts?

> AGENT

That ordering makes sense. Having both repos local before committing means ochid cross-references work from the start — no fixup needed.

The two-commit code repo is fine. Commit 1 is scaffolding (vc-config.toml, .gitignore, ochid), commit 2 adds the submodule. Clean and honest about what happened. Users who care can squash.

One practical question: when you do `git submodule add <github-url>`, does that work cleanly when `.claude/` already exists locally as a git repo in place? Or did you find you need to handle that specially (e.g., remove it first, let submodule add re-clone it, or use some flag)?

> DEVELOPER

I think it needs to be removed or be an empty directory, but either way it'll work. I'm hoping both repos end up with only a single commit "Initial Commit". IMO, we commit <name>.claude first then `rm -rf .claude/*` then do the `git submodule add ..` and finally commit <name>. That way a single "Initial Commit" for both repos.

> AGENT

That's clean. So the full flow is:

1. Create both repos locally, `git init` + `jj git init --colocate` each
2. Add `vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore` to both
3. Commit `.claude` repo ("Initial Commit" with ochid)
4. Create `.claude` GitHub repo, push it
5. `rm -rf .claude/*` (or remove and recreate empty)
6. `git submodule add <github-url>` in code repo — this populates `.claude` from the remote
7. Commit code repo ("Initial Commit" with ochid, includes `.gitmodules` + submodule ref)
8. Create code GitHub repo, push it
9. Symlink

One "Initial Commit" each, both with ochid cross-references, submodule in place from the first commit of the code repo. Ready to start implementing whenever you want — symlink fn first, then `new`.

1	# Chores-03
2	
3	## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)
4	
5	Port claude-symlink from to shell script below to a fn so
6	it can be used with a "new" command which will create the dual repos.
7	The base fn should probably not do any I/O it should return
8	approprate error information and other `fn` so the caller can
9	handle errors as needed.
10	```
11	#!/bin/bash
12	# claude-symlink.sh - Create a symlink for Claude Code project directories
13	# Authors: wink@saville.com and Claude Opus 4.5
14	
15	set -euo pipefail
16	
17	
18	usage() {
19	    echo "Usage: $(basename "$0") [-l] <target> [symlink-dir]" >&2
20	    echo "  -l          - List contents of symlinked directory after creation" >&2
21	    echo "  target      - Path to link to (e.g., ./.claude)" >&2
22	    echo "  symlink-dir - Directory for symlink (default: \$HOME/.claude/projects)" >&2
23	    exit 1
24	}
25	
26	list_contents=false
27	
28	while getopts ":l" opt; do
29	    case $opt in
30	        l) list_contents=true ;;
31	        *) usage ;;
32	    esac
33	done
34	shift $((OPTIND - 1))
35	
36	if [[ $# -lt 1 ]]; then
37	    usage
38	fi
39	
40	target="$1"
41	symlink_dir="${2:-$HOME/.claude/projects}"
42	
43	# Create target directory if it doesn't exist
44	if [[ ! -e "$target" ]]; then
45	    mkdir -p "$target"
46	    echo "Created target directory: $target"
47	fi
48	
49	# Resolve target to absolute path
50	abs_target="$(cd "$(dirname "$target")" && pwd)/$(basename "$target")"
51	
52	# Get current directory and convert to dash-separated name
53	# Claude Code encodes paths by replacing / and . with -
54	cwd="$(pwd)"
55	symlink_name="${cwd//\//-}"
56	symlink_name="${symlink_name//./-}"
57	
58	# Ensure symlink directory exists
59	mkdir -p "$symlink_dir"
60	
61	symlink_path="$symlink_dir/$symlink_name"
62	
63	# Check if something already exists at symlink path
64	if [[ -e "$symlink_path" || -L "$symlink_path" ]]; then
65	    if [[ -L "$symlink_path" ]]; then
66	        current_target="$(readlink "$symlink_path")"
67	        echo "Existing symlink: $symlink_path -> $current_target"
68	        read -rp "Replace with new target? [y/N] " response
69	        if [[ ! "$response" =~ ^[Yy]$ ]]; then
70	            echo "Aborted." >&2
71	            exit 1
72	        fi
73	    else
74	        echo "Error: '$symlink_path' exists and is not a symlink" >&2
75	        exit 1
76	    fi
77	fi
78	
79	# Create the symlink
80	ln -sfn "$abs_target" "$symlink_path"
81	
82	echo "Created: $symlink_path -> $abs_target"
83	
84	if [[ "$list_contents" == true ]]; then
85	    echo ""
86	    echo "Contents of $symlink_path:"
87	    ls -la "$symlink_path"/
88	fi
89	```
90

92

94	and the bot conversation. It will create a vc-config.toml file in both
95	repos and then use the `fn claude-symlink`. It should probably will use
96	`gh` commands, but a library is also an option, to create the repos
97	and then use `jj git init` for `jj` support.

102	with our dual repo system plus `fn claude-symlink` to be sure a symlink
103	exists. And then `jj git init` in each repo for `jj` support.

1	# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
2	
3	## Project Structure
4	
5	This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
6	
7	1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
8	2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
9	
10	Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
11	
12	## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
13	
14	- App repo: `.` (project root)
15	- Bot session repo: `.claude`
16	  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
17	
18	## Working Directory
19	
20	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
21	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
22	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
23	command failures downstream.
24	
25	## Committing
26	
27	Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
28	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
29	visible at the start of the command.
30	
31	### App repo
32	```
33	jj commit -m \
34	"title" \
35	-m "body
36	
37	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
38	-R .
39	```
40	
41	### Bot session repo
42	```
43	jj commit -m \
44	"title" \
45	-m "body
46	
47	ochid: /<changeID>" \
48	-R .claude
49	```
50	
51	## jj Basics
52	
53	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
54	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
55	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
56	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
57	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
58	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
59	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
60	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
61	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
62	  session because session data updates continuously.
63	
64	## Commit Message Style
65	
66	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
67	a version suffix:
68	
69	```
70	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
71	```
72	
73	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
74	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
75	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
76	- **Body**: expand on *what* if needed, plus short *why* and *how*.
77	- Examples:
78	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
79	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
80	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
81	
82	## Pre-commit Requirements
83	
84	### User approval
85	
86	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
87	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
88	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
89	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
90	
91	### Notes references
92	
93	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
94	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
95	
96	### Versioning
97	
98	Every change must start with a version bump. See
99	[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
100	for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
101	
102	### Pre-commit checklist
103	
104	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
105	
106	1. `cargo fmt`
107	2. `cargo clippy`
108	3. `cargo test`
109	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
110	5. Retest after install
111	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
112	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
113	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
114	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
115	
116	## ochid Trailers
117	
118	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
119	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
120	path followed by the changeID:
121	
122	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
123	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
124	
125	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
126	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
127	
128	## Session End Workflows
129	
130	When the user asks to "commit both repos" or says they're done, commit
131	both repos. Use the **same title** for both commits so they're easy to
132	correlate. The body can differ: the app repo body should summarize code
133	changes; the bot session repo body should note what was done in the
134	session.
135	
136	```
137	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
138	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
139	```
140	
141	After both commits, **pause and ask for user approval** before
142	proceeding to bookmark/push/finalize.
143	
144	When the user also asks to push, advance the current bookmark on both
145	repos, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude` here —
146	`finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
147	
148	Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
149	`dev-0.14.0`).
150	
151	```
152	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
153	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
154	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
155	```
156	
157	### Late changes after push
158	
159	If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
160	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
161	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
162	
163	```
164	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
165	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
166	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
167	```
168	
169	### Finalize the .claude repo
170	
171	The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
172	This squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The
173	delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
174	short relative path for `--repo`.
175	
176	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
177	calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
178	finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
179	
180	`--bookmark` is required — use the active bookmark for the session.
181	
182	```
183	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --push
184	```
185	
186	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
187	already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
188	miss the finalize squash window.
189	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	 
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore.
19	
20	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
21	
22	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
23	and using `jj-lib`
24	```
25	
26	## Versioning during development
27	
28	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
29	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
30	we go.
31	
32	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
33	
34	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
35	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
36	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
37	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
38	
39	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
40	
41	For multi-step:
42	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
43	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
44	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
45	
46	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
47	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
48	commits were exploratory vs final.
49	
50	## Todo format
51	
52	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
53	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
54	references.
55	
56	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
57	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
58	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
59	
60	Examples:
61	
62	# Todo
63	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
64	- Fix bug Y [1]
65	
66	# Done
67	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
68	
69	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
70	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
71	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
72	
```

1	# Chores-02
2	
3	## Graphical tools for jj repos
4	
5	### GG (gg)
6	
7	Cross-platform GUI for jj (Tauri-based). Interactive graph view.
8	
9	- Install: https://github.com/gulbanana/gg
10	- `gg gui` does NOT work; use `gg web` instead (opens in browser)
11	
12	### jj-fzf
13	
14	fzf-based TUI centered on `jj log` graph. Previews diffs, evolog,
15	op log. Many keybindings for common jj operations.
16	
17	- Install: https://github.com/tim-janik/jj-fzf
18	- Requires `fzf` installed separately (`sudo pacman -S fzf` on Arch)
19	
20	### Others (not tested)
21	
22	- **jjui** — TUI for jj (https://github.com/idursun/jjui)
23	- **jjgui** — commercial desktop app (https://jj-gui.com/)
24	- **Jujutsu Kaizen** — VS Code plugin
25	- **VisualJJ** — VS Code plugin (native jj, no git colocation)
26	- **Selvejj** — JetBrains plugin
27	
28	### Built-in
29	
30	- `jj log` — shows graph by default (no `--graph` flag needed)

  "new_string": "## Add `clone` command (0.29.0)\n\nThis command will clone the dual repos, probably using `gh`, or library,\nwith our dual repo system plus `fn claude-symlink` to be sure a symlink\nexists. And then `jj git init` in each repo for `jj` support.\n\n## Design for fn-claude-symlink, new, and clone\n\n### claude-symlink (fn)\n\nPort the bash script to a reusable Rust function. The function should\navoid doing I/O directly — return results/errors so callers can handle\nthem. This is the foundation for both `new` and `clone`.\n\n### new command\n\nMinimal initial implementation — create two repos with only:\n- `vc-config.toml`\n- `.gitignore`\n- `.jj/` and `.git/` (via `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`)\n- `.gitmodules` in the code repo (`.claude` as a git submodule)\n\nThe `.claude` repo is a git submodule of the code repo so that\n`git clone --recursive` can clone both at once.\n\n#### Order of operations\n\n1. Create both repos locally, `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`\n2. Add `vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` to both\n3. Commit `.claude` repo (\"Initial Commit\" with ochid cross-reference)\n4. Create `<name>.claude` GitHub repo via `gh`, push `.claude`\n5. Clean out `.claude` contents (so `git submodule add` can repopulate)\n6. `git submodule add <github-url-for-.claude>` in code repo\n7. Commit code repo (\"Initial Commit\" with ochid, `.gitmodules` + submodule ref)\n8. Create `<name>` GitHub repo via `gh`, push code repo\n9. Run symlink logic\n\nBoth repos end up with a single \"Initial Commit\", both with ochid\ncross-references. The submodule is present from the first commit of\nthe code repo.\n\n### clone command\n\nThin wrapper: `git clone --recursive` + `jj git init --colocate` in\nboth repos + symlink setup. Most of the heavy lifting is in `new`;\n`clone` is straightforward once the submodule relationship exists."

60	/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
61	///
62	/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
63	/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
64	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
65	pub struct DotSpec {
66	    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
67	    pub rev: String,
68	    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
69	    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
70	    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
71	    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
72	}
73	
74	/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
75	///
76	/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
77	/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
78	/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
79	/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
80	pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
81	    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
82	        DotSpec {
83	            rev: inner.to_string(),
84	            desc_count: None,
85	            anc_count: None,
86	        }
87	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
88	        DotSpec {
89	            rev: inner.to_string(),
90	            desc_count: None,
91	            anc_count: Some(0),
92	        }
93	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
94	        DotSpec {
95	            rev: inner.to_string(),
96	            desc_count: Some(0),
97	            anc_count: None,
98	        }
99	    } else {
100	        DotSpec {
101	            rev: rev.to_string(),
102	            desc_count: Some(0),
103	            anc_count: Some(0),
104	        }
105	    }
106	}
107	
108	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
109	///
110	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
111	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
112	pub fn resolve_spec(
113	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
114	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
115	    flag_rev: &str,
116	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
117	    default_rev: &str,
118	) -> DotSpec {
119	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
120	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
121	        flag_rev
122	    } else {
123	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev)
124	    };
125	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
126	
127	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
128	        flag_limit
129	    } else {
130	        pos_count
131	    };
132	
133	    if let Some(c) = count {
134	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
135	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
136	        if both_open {
137	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
138	            let desc_n = n / 2;
139	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
140	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
141	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
142	        } else {
143	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
144	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
145	            }
146	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
147	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
148	            }
149	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
150	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
151	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
152	            }
153	        }
154	    }
155	
156	    spec
157	}

173	/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
174	///
175	/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
176	pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
177	    if n == 0 {
178	        return s.to_string();
179	    }
180	    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
181	    let mut lines = s.lines();
182	    let mut result = match lines.next() {
183	        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
184	        None => return String::new(),
185	    };
186	    for line in lines {
187	        result.push('\n');
188	        if !line.is_empty() {
189	            result.push_str(&pad);
190	        }
191	        result.push_str(line);
192	    }
193	    result
194	}

196	/// Extract the ochid trailer value from a commit description, if present.
197	pub fn extract_ochid(commit: &Commit) -> Option<String> {
198	    for line in commit.description().lines().rev() {
199	        let trimmed = line.trim();
200	        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
201	            return Some(value.trim().to_string());
202	        }
203	    }
204	    None
205	}

207	/// Format: changeID ochid(padded) first-line (single line).
208	///
209	/// ochid column is left-padded to `width` characters. If no ochid, a blank
210	/// placeholder of that width is used.
211	pub fn format_commit_with_ochid(commit: &Commit, width: usize) -> String {
212	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
213	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
214	    let ochid = extract_ochid(commit).unwrap_or_default();
215	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
216	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
217	        "(no description set)"
218	    } else {
219	        first_line
220	    };
221	    format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {title}")
222	}
223	
224	/// Format: just the short changeID.
225	pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
226	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
227	    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
228	}
229	
230	/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
231	pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
232	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
233	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
234	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
235	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
236	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
237	    if first_line.is_empty() {
238	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
239	    } else {
240	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
241	    }
242	}
243	
244	/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
245	pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
246	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
247	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
248	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
249	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
250	    let desc = commit.description();
251	    if desc.is_empty() {
252	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
253	    } else {
254	        let mut lines = desc.lines();
255	        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
256	        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
257	        for line in lines {
258	            result.push('\n');
259	            result.push_str(line);
260	        }
261	        result
262	    }
263	}
264	
265	/// Collect commit IDs for `..x..` (both directions) or any dot notation.
266	///
267	/// Returns `(commit_ids, anchor_index)` — the IDs in display order
268	/// (descendants closest to anchor, anchor, ancestors closest to anchor)
269	/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.
270	///
271	/// - `desc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest descendants, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
272	/// - `anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
273	pub fn collect_ids(
274	    workspace: &Workspace,
275	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
276	    rev: &str,
277	    desc_count: Option<usize>,
278	    anc_count: Option<usize>,
279	) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
280	    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;
281	    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
282	        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{rev}'").into());
283	    }
284	    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
285	    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
286	
287	    // Descendants (closest to anchor)
288	    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
289	    if desc_count != Some(0) {
290	        let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{rev}::"))?;
291	        desc_ids = descendant_ids
292	            .into_iter()
293	            .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)
294	            .collect();
295	        if let Some(n) = desc_count {
296	            let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(n);
297	            desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
298	        }
299	    }
300	
301	    // Ancestors (closest to anchor)
302	    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
303	    if anc_count != Some(0) {
304	        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{rev}"))?;
305	        let limit = anc_count.unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
306	        let mut count = 0;
307	        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {
308	            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {
309	                continue;
310	            }
311	            anc_ids.push(commit_id);
312	            count += 1;
313	            if count >= limit {
314	                break;
315	            }
316	        }
317	    }
318	
319	    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();
320	    let mut result = desc_ids;
321	    result.push(anchor_id.clone());
322	    result.extend(anc_ids);
323	    Ok((result, anchor_index))
324	}

326	/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.
327	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
328	pub enum Header {
329	    /// Show `deco path deco` header per repo.
330	    Label(String),
331	    /// No header at all.
332	    None,
333	}
334	
335	/// Resolve `-l` / `-L` flags into a `Header`.
336	///
337	/// `-L` (no header) takes precedence over `-l` (label decoration).
338	pub fn resolve_header(label: &str, suppress: bool) -> Header {
339	    if suppress {
340	        Header::None
341	    } else {
342	        Header::Label(label.to_string())
343	    }
344	}
345	
346	/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `["."]`, and run a closure for each.
347	///
348	/// Header style controls output between repos.
349	/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.
350	pub fn for_each_repo<F>(
351	    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],
352	    header: &Header,
353	    mut body: F,
354	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
355	where
356	    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
357	{
358	    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {
359	        vec![PathBuf::from(".")]
360	    } else {
361	        raw_repos
362	            .iter()
363	            .flat_map(|p| {
364	                p.to_string_lossy()
365	                    .split(',')
366	                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
367	                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
368	            })
369	            .collect()
370	    };
371	    let multi = repos.len() > 1;
372	    let mut first = true;
373	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
374	
375	    for repo_path in &repos {
376	        if multi {
377	            match header {
378	                Header::Label(deco) => {
379	                    if !first {
380	                        println!();
381	                    }
382	                    println!(
383	                        "{}",
384	                        bold(&format!("{deco} {} {deco}", repo_path.display()))
385	                    );
386	                }
387	                Header::None => {}
388	            }
389	        }
390	        first = false;
391	
392	        match load_repo(repo_path) {
393	            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {
394	                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {
395	                    eprintln!("error ({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
396	                    errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
397	                }
398	            }
399	            Err(e) => {
400	                eprintln!("error ({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
401	                errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
402	            }
403	        }
404	    }
405	
406	    if errors.is_empty() {
407	        Ok(())
408	    } else {
409	        Err(format!("{} repo(s) had errors", errors.len()).into())
410	    }
411	}
412	
413	pub fn load_repo(
414	    path: &Path,
415	) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
416	    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
417	    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
418	    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
419	    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
420	
421	    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
422	    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
423	
424	    Ok((workspace, repo))
425	}
426	
427	/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
428	pub fn resolve_revset(
429	    workspace: &Workspace,
430	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
431	    revset_str: &str,
432	) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
433	    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
434	    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
435	    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
436	    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
437	    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
438	        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
439	        base: workspace_root,
440	    };
441	    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
442	    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
443	        path_converter: &path_converter,
444	        workspace_name,
445	    };
446	    let context = RevsetParseContext {
447	        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
448	        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
449	        user_email: "",
450	        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
451	        default_ignored_remote: None,
452	        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
453	        use_glob_by_default: false,
454	        extensions: &extensions,
455	        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
456	    };
457	
458	    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
459	    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
460	
461	    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
462	    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
463	    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
464	    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
465	
466	    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
467	    for result in revset.iter() {
468	        commit_ids.push(result?);
469	    }
470	    Ok(commit_ids)
471	}

1	use std::collections::HashMap;
2	use std::path::Path;
3	
4	/// Load a TOML file into a flat key-value map.
5	///
6	/// Handles `[section]` headers, bare `key = "value"` pairs, comments, and
7	/// blank lines. Keys under a section are stored as `section.key`. Quoted
8	/// string values have their quotes stripped; unquoted values are stored
9	/// as-is.
10	///
11	/// This is intentionally minimal — just enough for `.vc-config.toml`.
12	pub fn toml_load(path: &Path) -> Result<HashMap<String, String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
13	    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
14	        .map_err(|e| format!("cannot read '{}': {e}", path.display()))?;
15	
16	    let mut map = HashMap::new();
17	    let mut section = String::new();
18	
19	    for line in content.lines() {
20	        let trimmed = line.trim();
21	
22	        // Skip blank lines and comments
23	        if trimmed.is_empty() || trimmed.starts_with('#') {
24	            continue;
25	        }
26	
27	        // Section header: [name]
28	        if let Some(inner) = trimmed.strip_prefix('[').and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(']')) {
29	            section = inner.trim().to_string();
30	            continue;
31	        }
32	
33	        // Key = value
34	        if let Some((key, value)) = trimmed.split_once('=') {
35	            let key = key.trim();
36	            let value = value.trim();
37	
38	            // Strip surrounding quotes from string values
39	            let value = if (value.starts_with('"') && value.ends_with('"'))
40	                || (value.starts_with('\'') && value.ends_with('\''))
41	            {
42	                &value[1..value.len() - 1]
43	            } else {
44	                value
45	            };
46	
47	            let full_key = if section.is_empty() {
48	                key.to_string()
49	            } else {
50	                format!("{section}.{key}")
51	            };
52	
53	            map.insert(full_key, value.to_string());
54	        }
55	    }
56	
57	    Ok(map)
58	}
59	
60	/// Look up a dotted key (e.g. `"workspace.path"`) in a loaded config map.
61	pub fn toml_get<'a>(map: &'a HashMap<String, String>, key: &str) -> Option<&'a String> {
62	    map.get(key)
63	}
64

  "content": "use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\nuse clap::Args;\n\n/// What action the symlink operation needs to take.\n#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SymlinkAction {\n    /// Nothing exists at the symlink path — create it.\n    Create,\n    /// An existing symlink points somewhere else — caller must decide whether to replace.\n    Replace { current_target: PathBuf },\n    /// The symlink already points to the correct target — nothing to do.\n    AlreadyCorrect,\n}\n\n/// The result of planning a symlink operation.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct SymlinkPlan {\n    /// Absolute path to the target directory (what the symlink points to).\n    pub abs_target: PathBuf,\n    /// Full path of the symlink to create/replace.\n    pub symlink_path: PathBuf,\n    /// What action is needed.\n    pub action: SymlinkAction,\n}\n\n/// Encode a path the way Claude Code does: replace `/` and `.` with `-`.\npub fn encode_path(path: &str) -> String {\n    path.replace('/', \"-\").replace('.', \"-\")\n}\n\n/// Compute the symlink plan without performing any I/O.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n/// * `cwd` — current working directory (used to derive the symlink name)\n/// * `target` — the directory the symlink should point to (resolved to absolute)\n/// * `symlink_dir` — parent directory for the symlink (e.g. `~/.claude/projects`)\n/// * `target_exists` — whether the target directory exists on disk\n/// * `symlink_meta` — what currently exists at the symlink path:\n///   - `None` — nothing exists\n///   - `Some(None)` — exists but is not a symlink (file or directory)\n///   - `Some(Some(path))` — existing symlink pointing to `path`\npub fn compute_plan(\n    cwd: &Path,\n    target: &Path,\n    symlink_dir: &Path,\n    symlink_meta: Option<Option<PathBuf>>,\n) -> Result<SymlinkPlan, String> {\n    // Resolve target to absolute path\n    let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {\n        target.to_path_buf()\n    } else {\n        cwd.join(target)\n    };\n\n    // Derive symlink name from cwd\n    let cwd_str = cwd\n        .to_str()\n        .ok_or_else(|| \"current directory path is not valid UTF-8\".to_string())?;\n    let symlink_name = encode_path(cwd_str);\n    let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(symlink_name);\n\n    let action = match symlink_meta {\n        None => SymlinkAction::Create,\n        Some(None) => {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"'{}' exists and is not a symlink\",\n                symlink_path.display()\n            ));\n        }\n        Some(Some(current_target)) => {\n            if current_target == abs_target {\n                SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect\n            } else {\n                SymlinkAction::Replace { current_target }\n            }\n        }\n    };\n\n    Ok(SymlinkPlan {\n        abs_target,\n        symlink_path,\n        action,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Read what exists at a path without following symlinks.\n///\n/// Returns:\n/// - `None` — nothing exists\n/// - `Some(None)` — exists but is not a symlink\n/// - `Some(Some(target))` — symlink pointing to target\npub fn probe_symlink(path: &Path) -> Option<Option<PathBuf>> {\n    match path.symlink_metadata() {\n        Err(_) => None,\n        Ok(meta) => {\n            if meta.is_symlink() {\n                Some(Some(std::fs::read_link(path).unwrap_or_default()))\n            } else {\n                Some(None)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Execute a symlink plan: create the target dir if needed, create/replace the symlink.\npub fn execute_plan(plan: &SymlinkPlan, create_target: bool) -> Result<(), String> {\n    if create_target && !plan.abs_target.exists() {\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&plan.abs_target)\n            .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot create target '{}': {e}\", plan.abs_target.display()))?;\n    }\n\n    // Ensure symlink parent directory exists\n    if let Some(parent) = plan.symlink_path.parent() {\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| {\n            format!(\n                \"cannot create symlink directory '{}': {e}\",\n                parent.display()\n            )\n        })?;\n    }\n\n    match &plan.action {\n        SymlinkAction::Create => {}\n        SymlinkAction::Replace { .. } => {\n            std::fs::remove_file(&plan.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {\n                format!(\n                    \"cannot remove existing symlink '{}': {e}\",\n                    plan.symlink_path.display()\n                )\n            })?;\n        }\n        SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect => return Ok(()),\n    }\n\n    #[cfg(unix)]\n    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&plan.abs_target, &plan.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {\n        format!(\n            \"cannot create symlink '{}' -> '{}': {e}\",\n            plan.symlink_path.display(),\n            plan.abs_target.display()\n        )\n    })?;\n\n    #[cfg(not(unix))]\n    return Err(\"symlink creation is only supported on Unix\".to_string());\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n// -- Subcommand --\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SymlinkArgs {\n    /// Directory to link to [default: .claude]\n    #[arg(value_name = \"TARGET\")]\n    pub target: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Directory for symlink [default: ~/.claude/projects]\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"PATH\")]\n    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// List contents of symlinked directory after creation\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub list: bool,\n\n    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub yes: bool,\n}\n\npub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n\n    let target = match &args.target {\n        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),\n        None => PathBuf::from(\".claude\"),\n    };\n\n    let symlink_dir = match &args.symlink_dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => {\n            let home = std::env::var(\"HOME\")\n                .map_err(|_| \"HOME environment variable not set\".to_string())?;\n            PathBuf::from(home).join(\".claude\").join(\"projects\")\n        }\n    };\n\n    let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {\n        target.clone()\n    } else {\n        cwd.join(&target)\n    };\n\n    let symlink_name = encode_path(\n        cwd.to_str()\n            .ok_or(\"current directory path is not valid UTF-8\")?,\n    );\n    let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(&symlink_name);\n\n    let meta = probe_symlink(&symlink_path);\n    let plan = compute_plan(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir, meta)?;\n\n    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement\n    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = plan.action {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"Existing symlink: {} -> {}\",\n            plan.symlink_path.display(),\n            current_target.display()\n        );\n        if !args.yes {\n            eprint!(\"Replace with new target? [y/N] \");\n            let mut response = String::new();\n            std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;\n            if !response.trim().eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"y\") {\n                return Err(\"aborted\".into());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if plan.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {\n        println!(\n            \"Already correct: {} -> {}\",\n            plan.symlink_path.display(),\n            plan.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    } else {\n        execute_plan(&plan, true)?;\n        println!(\n            \"Created: {} -> {}\",\n            plan.symlink_path.display(),\n            plan.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    if args.list {\n        println!();\n        println!(\"Contents of {}:\", abs_target.display());\n        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&abs_target)? {\n            let entry = entry?;\n            println!(\"  {}\", entry.file_name().to_string_lossy());\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn encode_path_basic() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            encode_path(\"/home/wink/data/prgs/vc-x1\"),\n            \"-home-wink-data-prgs-vc-x1\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn encode_path_with_dots() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            encode_path(\"/home/wink/.config/test\"),\n            \"-home-wink--config-test\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn plan_create_when_nothing_exists() {\n        let cwd = Path::new(\"/home/user/project\");\n        let target = Path::new(\".claude\");\n        let symlink_dir = Path::new(\"/home/user/.claude/projects\");\n\n        let plan = compute_plan(cwd, target, symlink_dir, None).unwrap();\n\n        assert_eq!(plan.abs_target, PathBuf::from(\"/home/user/project/.claude\"));\n        assert_eq!(\n            plan.symlink_path,\n 167	    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting
168	    #[arg(short, long)]
169	    pub yes: bool,
170	}
171	
172	pub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
173	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
174	
175	    let target = match &args.target {
176	        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),
177	        None => PathBuf::from(".claude"),
178	    };
179	
180	    let symlink_dir = match &args.symlink_dir {
181	        Some(d) => d.clone(),
182	        None => {
183	            let home = std::env::var("HOME")
184	                .map_err(|_| "HOME environment variable not set".to_string())?;
185	            PathBuf::from(home).join(".claude").join("projects")
186	        }
187	    };
188	
189	    let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
190	        target.clone()
191	    } else {
192	        cwd.join(&target)
193	    };
194	
195	    let symlink_name = encode_path(
196	        cwd.to_str()
197	            .ok_or("current directory path is not valid UTF-8")?,
198	    );
199	    let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(&symlink_name);
200	
201	    let meta = probe_symlink(&symlink_path);
202	    let plan = compute_plan(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir, meta)?;
203	
204	    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement
205	    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = plan.action {
206	        eprintln!(
207	            "Existing symlink: {} -> {}",
208	            plan.symlink_path.display(),
209	            current_target.display()
210	        );
211	        if !args.yes {
212	            eprint!("Replace with new target? [y/N] ");
213	            let mut response = String::new();
214	            std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;
215	            if !response.trim().eq_ignore_ascii_case("y") {
216	                return Err("aborted".into());
217	            }
218	        }
219	    }
220	
221	    if plan.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {
222	        println!(
223	            "Already correct: {} -> {}",
224	            plan.symlink_path.display(),
225	            plan.abs_target.display()
226	        );
227	    } else {
228	        execute_plan(&plan, true)?;
229	        println!(
230	            "Created: {} -> {}",
231	            plan.symlink_path.display(),
232	            plan.abs_target.display()
233	        );
234	    }
235	
236	    if args.list {
237	        println!();
238	        println!("Contents of {}:", abs_target.display());
239	        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&abs_target)? {
240	            let entry = entry?;
241	            println!("  {}", entry.file_name().to_string_lossy());
242	        }
243	    }
244	
245	    Ok(())
246	}
247

  "old_string": "## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)\n\nPort claude-symlink from to shell script below to a fn so\nit can be used with a \"new\" command which will create the dual repos.\nThe base fn should probably not do any I/O it should return\napproprate error information and other `fn` so the caller can\nhandle errors as needed.",
  "new_string": "## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)\n\nPort claude-symlink from shell script below to a fn so\nit can be used with a \"new\" command which will create the dual repos.\nThe base fn should probably not do any I/O it should return\nappropriate error information and other `fn` so the caller can\nhandle errors as needed.\n\n### Implementation\n\nAdded `src/symlink.rs` with a pure-logic / I/O separation:\n\n- `encode_path()` — encodes a path the Claude Code way (`/` and `.` → `-`)\n- `compute_plan()` — pure function, returns a `SymlinkPlan` with action\n  (`Create`, `Replace`, `AlreadyCorrect`, or error) without touching the filesystem\n- `probe_symlink()` — reads what exists at a path (nothing, file, or symlink)\n- `execute_plan()` — creates/replaces the symlink per the plan\n- `symlink()` — subcommand handler with interactive prompt for replacement\n\nCLI: `vc-x1 symlink [TARGET] [--symlink-dir PATH] [-l] [-y]`"
}
```

8	pub struct FinalizeArgs {
9	    /// Path to jj repo
10	    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
11	    pub repo: PathBuf,
12	
13	    /// Source revision to squash
14	    #[arg(long, default_value = "@")]
15	    pub source: String,
16	
17	    /// Target revision to squash into
18	    #[arg(long, default_value = "@-")]
19	    pub target: String,
20	
21	    /// Seconds to wait before squashing
22	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0)]
23	    pub delay: f64,
24	
25	    /// Bookmark to advance to target after squash (required)
26	    #[arg(long)]
27	    pub bookmark: String,
28	
29	    /// Push after squashing
30	    #[arg(long)]
31	    pub push: bool,
32	
33	    /// Log file path (default: /tmp/vc-x1-finalize-<timestamp-millis>.log)
34	    #[arg(long)]

36	
37	    /// Detach and run in the background
38	    #[arg(long)]
39	    pub detach: bool,
40	
41	    /// Internal: run exec path (used by detach)
42	    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
43	    pub exec: bool,
44	}
45	
46	#[derive(Debug)]
47	pub struct FinalizeOpts {
48	    pub repo: PathBuf,
49	    pub source: String,
50	    pub target: String,
51	    pub bookmark: String,
52	    pub delay_secs: f64,
53	    pub push: bool,
54	    pub log: PathBuf,
55	    pub detach: bool,
56	    pub exec: bool,
57	}
58	
59	impl FinalizeArgs {
60	    pub fn into_opts(self) -> FinalizeOpts {
61	        let log = self.log.unwrap_or_else(|| {
62	            PathBuf::from(format!(
63	                "/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-{}.log",
64	                std::time::SystemTime::now()
65	                    .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
66	                    .unwrap_or_default()
67	                    .as_millis()
68	            ))
69	        });
70	        FinalizeOpts {
71	            repo: self.repo,
72	            source: self.source,
73	            target: self.target,
74	            bookmark: self.bookmark,
75	            delay_secs: self.delay,
76	            push: self.push,
77	            log,
78	            detach: self.detach,
79	            exec: self.exec,
80	        }
81	    }
82	}
83	
84	pub fn log_msg(log: &Path, msg: &str) {
85	    let nanos = std::time::SystemTime::now()
86	        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
87	        .unwrap_or_default()
88	        .as_nanos();
89	    let pid = std::process::id();
90	    let line = format!("[{nanos}] pid={pid} {msg}\n");
91	    if let Ok(mut f) = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
92	        .create(true)
93	        .append(true)
94	        .open(log)
95	    {
96	        let _ = f.write_all(line.as_bytes());
97	    }
98	}
99	
100	fn run_jj(args: &[&str], log: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
101	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
102	    log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: jj {args_str}"));
103	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
104	        .args(args)
105	        .output()
106	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj: {e}"))?;
107	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
108	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
109	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
110	        log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: stdout: {stdout}"));
111	    }
112	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
113	        log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: stderr: {stderr}"));
114	    }
115	    if !output.status.success() {
116	        return Err(format!("jj {args_str} failed (exit {}): {stderr}", output.status).into());
117	    }
118	    Ok(())
119	}
120	
121	fn finalize_exec(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
122	    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize_exec: starting opts={opts:?}"));
123	
124	    // Sleep to let trailing writes settle
125	    let delay = std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(opts.delay_secs);
126	    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize_exec: sleeping {delay:?}"));
127	    std::thread::sleep(delay);
128	
129	    // Squash source into target
130	    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();
131	    run_jj(
132	        &[
133	            "squash",
134	            "--ignore-immutable",
135	            "--use-destination-message",
136	            "--from",
137	            &opts.source,
138	            "--into",
139	            &opts.target,
140	            "-R",
141	            &repo_str,
142	        ],
143	        &opts.log,
144	    )?;
145	
146	    // Advance bookmark to target
147	    run_jj(
148	        &[
149	            "bookmark",
150	            "set",
151	            &opts.bookmark,
152	            "-r",
153	            &opts.target,
154	            "-R",
155	            &repo_str,
156	        ],
157	        &opts.log,
158	    )?;
159	
160	    if opts.push {
161	        run_jj(
162	            &["git", "push", "--bookmark", &opts.bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
163	            &opts.log,
164	        )?;
165	    }
166	
167	    log_msg(&opts.log, "finalize_exec: done");
168	    Ok(())
169	}
170	
171	pub fn build_exec_args(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Vec<String> {
172	    let mut args = vec![
173	        "finalize".to_string(),
174	        "--exec".to_string(),
175	        "--repo".to_string(),
176	        opts.repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
177	        "--source".to_string(),
178	        opts.source.clone(),
179	        "--target".to_string(),
180	        opts.target.clone(),
181	        "--bookmark".to_string(),
182	        opts.bookmark.clone(),
183	        "--delay".to_string(),
184	        opts.delay_secs.to_string(),
185	        "--log".to_string(),
186	        opts.log.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
187	    ];
188	    if opts.push {
189	        args.push("--push".to_string());
190	    }
191	    args
192	}
193	
194	/// Spawn a detached child process with `--exec` and return immediately.
195	/// The child re-enters `main()`, parses `--exec`, and `finalize()` routes
196	/// it to `finalize_exec()` where the actual work happens.
197	fn detach(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
198	    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("detach: parent starting opts={opts:?}"));
199	
200	    let exe = std::env::current_exe()?;
201	    let args = build_exec_args(opts);
202	    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("detach: exe={exe:?} args={args:?}"));
203	
204	    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(exe);
205	    cmd.args(&args).stdin(Stdio::null());
206	
207	    #[cfg(unix)]
208	    {
209	        use std::os::unix::process::CommandExt;
210	        unsafe {
211	            cmd.pre_exec(|| {
212	                libc::setsid();
213	                Ok(())
214	            });
215	        }
216	    }
217	
218	    let child = cmd.spawn()?;
219	    log_msg(
220	        &opts.log,
221	        &format!("detach: spawned child pid={}", child.id()),
222	    );
223	    eprintln!(
224	        "finalize: detached (pid {}), log: {}",
225	        child.id(),
226	        opts.log.display()
227	    );
228	    Ok(())
229	}
230	
231	pub fn finalize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
232	    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize: entry opts={opts:?}"));
233	    let result = if opts.detach && !opts.exec {
234	        detach(opts)
235	    } else {
236	        finalize_exec(opts)
237	    };
238	    match &result {
239	        Ok(()) => log_msg(&opts.log, "finalize: exit ok"),
240	        Err(e) => log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize: exit err={e}")),
241	    }
242	    result
243	}
