  "content": "use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\nuse clap::Args;\n\nuse crate::symlink;\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct NewArgs {\n    /// Project name (directory and GitHub repo name)\n    #[arg(value_name = \"NAME\")]\n    pub name: String,\n\n    /// GitHub user or organization\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub owner: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Parent directory to create project in [default: cwd]\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// Create private GitHub repos (default: public)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub private: bool,\n\n    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dry_run: bool,\n}\n\n/// Run a command, printing it first. Returns stdout on success.\nfn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let args_str = args.join(\" \");\n    eprintln!(\"  $ {cmd} {args_str}\");\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(cwd)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run {cmd}: {e}\"))?;\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n        return Err(format!(\"{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    Ok(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string())\n}\n\n/// Get the current GitHub user via `gh api user`.\nfn gh_whoami() -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(\"gh\")\n        .args([\"api\", \"user\", \"--jq\", \".login\"])\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run gh: {e}\"))?;\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n        return Err(format!(\"gh api user failed (is gh authenticated?): {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    Ok(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string())\n}\n\n/// Check if a GitHub repo exists.\nfn gh_repo_exists(owner: &str, name: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let full = format!(\"{owner}/{name}\");\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(\"gh\")\n        .args([\"repo\", \"view\", &full])\n        .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())\n        .stderr(std::process::Stdio::null())\n        .status()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run gh: {e}\"))?;\n    Ok(output.success())\n}\n\nconst VC_CONFIG_CODE: &str = r#\"# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration\n#\n# workspace-path is this repo's path relative to the workspace root.\n# Used to resolve changeID paths in git trailers (e.g. ochid: /changeID).\n# other-repo is the relative path to the counterpart repo.\n\n[workspace]\npath = \"/\"\nother-repo = \".claude\"\n\"#;\n\nconst VC_CONFIG_SESSION: &str = r#\"# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration\n#\n# workspace-path is this repo's path relative to the workspace root.\n# Used to resolve changeID paths in git trailers (e.g. ochid: /.claude/changeID).\n# other-repo is the relative path to the counterpart repo.\n\n[workspace]\npath = \"/.claude\"\nother-repo = \"..\"\n\"#;\n\nconst GITIGNORE_CODE: &str = \"/target\n/.claude\n/.git\n/.jj\n\";\n\nconst GITIGNORE_SESSION: &str = \".git\n.jj\n\";\n\n/// Get the jj changeID for the working copy in a repo.\nfn jj_change_id(repo_path: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\"log\", \"-r\", \"@\", \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"change_id\", \"--limit\", \"1\"])\n        .current_dir(repo_path)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run jj: {e}\"))?;\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n        return Err(format!(\"jj log failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    let full_id = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n    // Return short form (first 12 chars)\n    Ok(full_id[..full_id.len().min(12)].to_string())\n}\n\npub fn new(args: &NewArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let parent_dir = match &args.dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => std::env::current_dir()?,\n    };\n    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&args.name);\n    let session_dir = project_dir.join(\".claude\");\n\n    // Preflight checks\n    eprintln!(\"Preflight checks...\");\n\n    // Check tools\n    std::process::Command::new(\"gh\")\n        .arg(\"auth\")\n        .arg(\"status\")\n        .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())\n        .stderr(std::process::Stdio::null())\n        .status()\n        .map_err(|_| \"gh is not installed\")?\n        .success()\n        .then_some(())\n        .ok_or(\"gh is not authenticated (run: gh auth login)\")?;\n\n    std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .arg(\"--version\")\n        .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())\n        .status()\n        .map_err(|_| \"jj is not installed\")?;\n\n    let owner = match &args.owner {\n        Some(o) => o.clone(),\n        None => gh_whoami()?,\n    };\n\n    if project_dir.exists() {\n        return Err(format!(\"'{}' already exists\", project_dir.display()).into());\n    }\n\n    let code_repo = format!(\"{owner}/{}\", args.name);\n    let session_repo = format!(\"{owner}/{}.claude\", args.name);\n\n    if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &args.name)? {\n        return Err(format!(\"GitHub repo '{code_repo}' already exists\").into());\n    }\n    if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &format!(\"{}.claude\", args.name))? {\n        return Err(format!(\"GitHub repo '{session_repo}' already exists\").into());\n    }\n\n    let visibility = if args.private { \"--private\" } else { \"--public\" };\n\n    if args.dry_run {\n        eprintln!(\"\\nDry run — would execute:\");\n        eprintln!(\"  1. Create directories: {}\", project_dir.display());\n        eprintln!(\"  2. git init + jj git init --colocate in both repos\");\n        eprintln!(\"  3. Add .vc-config.toml and .gitignore to both\");\n        eprintln!(\"  4. Commit .claude repo with ochid\");\n        eprintln!(\"  5. gh repo create {session_repo} {visibility}\");\n        eprintln!(\"  6. Push .claude, clean contents, git submodule add\");\n        eprintln!(\"  7. Commit code repo with ochid + .gitmodules\");\n        eprintln!(\"  8. gh repo create {code_repo} {visibility}\");\n        eprintln!(\"  9. Push code repo\");\n        eprintln!(\"  10. Create Claude Code symlink\");\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    // Step 1: Create directories\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCreating project directories...\");\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 2: git init + jj init in both repos\n    eprintln!(\"\\nInitializing repos...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], &session_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 3: Write config files\n    eprintln!(\"\\nWriting config files...\");\n    std::fs::write(project_dir.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), VC_CONFIG_CODE)?;\n    std::fs::write(project_dir.join(\".gitignore\"), GITIGNORE_CODE)?;\n    std::fs::write(session_dir.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), VC_CONFIG_SESSION)?;\n    std::fs::write(session_dir.join(\".gitignore\"), GITIGNORE_SESSION)?;\n\n    // Step 4: Commit .claude repo first (need its changeID for code repo ochid)\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCommitting session repo...\");\n    // Get the code repo's changeID before committing .claude\n    let code_chid = jj_change_id(&project_dir)?;\n    let session_commit_body = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"commit\", \"-m\", &session_commit_body],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Now get the .claude repo's committed changeID for the code repo ochid\n    // After jj commit, the committed revision is @-\n    let session_chid_output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\"log\", \"-r\", \"@-\", \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"change_id\", \"--limit\", \"1\"])\n        .current_dir(&session_dir)\n        .output()?;\n    let session_chid_full = String::from_utf8_lossy(&session_chid_output.stdout)\n        .trim()\n        .to_string();\n    let session_chid = &session_chid_full[..session_chid_full.len().min(12)];\n\n    // Step 5: Create .claude GitHub repo and push\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCreating GitHub repo {session_repo}...\");\n    run(\n        \"gh\",\n        &[\"repo\", \"create\", &session_repo, visibility],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    let session_url = format!(\"git@github.com:{session_repo}.git\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"remote\", \"add\", \"origin\", &session_url], &session_dir)?;\n    // Set the bookmark before pushing\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &session_dir)?;\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"git\", \"push\", \"--bookmark\", \"main\"],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 6: Clean .claude contents and add as submodule\n    eprintln!(\"\\nAdding .claude as submodule...\");\n    // Remove the .claude directory contents so git submodule add can work\n    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&session_dir)?;\n\n    run(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"submodule\", \"add\", &session_url, \".claude\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Re-init jj in the submodule (git submodule add cloned fresh)\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 7: Commit code repo\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCommitting code repo...\");\n    let code_commit_body = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"commit\", \"-m\", &code_commit_body],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 8: Create code GitHub repo and push\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCreating GitHub repo {code_repo}...\");\n    run(\n        \"gh\",\n        &[\"repo\", \"create\", &code_repo, visibility],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    let code_url = format!(\"git@github.com:{code_repo}.git\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"remote\", \"add\", \"origin\", &code_url], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"git\", \"push\", \"--bookmark\", \"main\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 9: Create Claude Code symlink\n    eprintln!(\"\\nCreating Claude Code symlink...\");\n    let symlink_dir = {\n        let home =\n            std::env::var(\"HOME\").map_err(|_| \"HOME environment variable not set\".to_string())?;\n        PathBuf::from(home).join(\".claude\").join(\"projects\")\n    };\n\n    let meta = symlink::probe_symlink(\n        &symlink_dir.join(symlink::encode_path(\n            project_dir\n                .to_str()\n                .ok_or(\"project path is not valid UTF-8\")?,\n        )),\n    );\n    let plan = symlink::compute_plan(&project_dir, Path::new(\".claude\"), &symlink_dir, meta)?;\n    symlink::execute_plan(&plan, false)?;\n    eprintln!(\n        \"  Symlink: {} -> {}\",\n        plan.symlink_path.display(),\n        plan.abs_target.display()\n    );\n\n    eprintln!(\"\\nDone! Project created at {}\", project_dir.display());\n    eprintln!(\"  Code repo:    {code_repo}\");\n    eprintln!(\"  Session repo: {session_repo}\");\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::{Cli, Commands};\n    use clap::Parser;\n\n    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> NewArgs {\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();\n        match cli.command {\n            Commands::New(a) => a,\n            _ => panic!(\"expected New\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn parse_err(args: &[&str]) -> String {\n        Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap_err().to_string()\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn defaults() {\n        let args = parse(&[\"vc-x1\", \"new\", \"my-project\"]);\n        assert_eq!(args.name, \"my-project\");\n        assert!(args.owner.is_none());\n        assert!(args.dir.is_none());\n        assert!(!args.private);\n        assert!(!args.dry_run);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn all_opts() {\n        let args = parse(&[\n            \"vc-x1\",\n            \"new\",\n            \"my-project\",\n            \"--owner\",\n            \"myorg\",\n            \"--dir\",\n            \"/tmp/projects\",\n            \"--private\",\n            \"--dry-run\",\n        ]);\n        assert_eq!(args.name, \"my-project\");\n        assert_eq!(args.owner.as_deref(), Some(\"myorg\"));\n        assert_eq!(args.dir, Some(PathBuf::from(\"/tmp/projects\")));\n        assert!(args.private);\n        assert!(args.dry_run);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn missing_name() {\n        let err = parse_err(&[\"vc-x1\", \"new\"]);\n        assert!(err.contains(\"NAME\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn config_content_code() {\n        assert!(VC_CONFIG_CODE.contains(\"path = \\\"/\\\"\"));\n        assert!(VC_CONFIG_CODE.contains(\"other-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn config_content_session() {\n        assert!(VC_CONFIG_SESSION.contains(\"path = \\\"/.claude\\\"\"));\n        assert!(VC_CONFIG_SESSION.contains(\"other-repo = \\\"..\\\"\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn gitignore_code_excludes_claude() {\n        assert!(GITIGNORE_CODE.contains(\"/.claude\"));\n        assert!(GITIGNORE_CODE.contains(\"/.git\"));\n        assert!(GITIGNORE_CODE.contains(\"/.jj\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn gitignore_session_excludes_git() {\n        assert!(GITIGNORE_SESSION.contains(\".git\"));\n        assert!(GITIGNORE_SESSION.contains(\".jj\"));\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "## Add `new` command (0.28.0)\n\nThis command will create the dual repos use by vc-x1 to hold the code\nand the bot conversation. It will create a vc-config.toml file in both\nrepos and then use the `fn claude-symlink`.   "new_string": "## Add `new` command (0.28.0)\n\nThis command will create the dual repos use by vc-x1 to hold the code\nand the bot conversation. It will create a vc-config.toml file in both\nrepos and then use the `fn claude-symlink`. It should probably will use\n`gh` commands, but a library is also an option, to create the repos\nand then use `jj git init` for `jj` support.\n\n### Implementation\n\nAdded `src/new.rs` — orchestrates dual-repo creation via `git`, `jj`, and `gh` CLI.\n\nCLI: `vc-x1 new <NAME> [--owner OWNER] [--dir PATH] [--private] [--dry-run]`\n\nFlow:\n1. Preflight: verify `gh` auth, `jj` installed, no local/remote conflicts\n2. Create both repos locally with `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`\n3. Write `.vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` to both\n4. Commit `.claude` repo (\"Initial commit\" with ochid to code repo)\n5. Create `<name>.claude` GitHub repo, push\n6. Remove `.claude` contents, `git submodule add` re-clones it\n7. Commit code repo (\"Initial commit\" with ochid + `.gitmodules`)\n8. Create `<name>` GitHub repo, push\n9. Create Claude Code symlink\n\nBoth repos end up with a single \"Initial commit\" with ochid cross-references."
}

> DEVELOPER

Actually, I'm thinking maybe `fn new` is not a great name as it's convention is making a "new" instance I wonder if we should use another name, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good point — `new` in Rust idiom means constructor. Some alternatives:

- `create` — direct, describes the action
- `init` — common for project scaffolding (like `git init`, `cargo init`)
- `setup` — though that's already a planned subcommand for something else

I'd lean toward `init` since it's creating a fresh project from scratch, same as `cargo init` or `jj git init`. `create` is also fine but more generic. What's your preference?

> DEVELOPER

let's go with init

```
1	# vc-x1
2	
3	- [Overview](#vc-x1)
4	- [Usage](#usage)
5	  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
6	  - [Shell completion](#shell-completion)
7	  - [validate-desc](#validate-desc)
8	  - [fix-desc](#fix-desc)
9	  - [finalize](#finalize)
10	  - [Testing finalize](#testing-finalize)
11	- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
12	- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
13	- [License](#license)
14	
15	This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
16	We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
17	in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
18	initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
19	made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
20	and one with the conversation with the bot.
21	
22	I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
23	each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
24	of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
25	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
26	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
27	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
28	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
29	
30	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
31	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
32	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
33	own IDE for vc.
34	
35	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
36	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
37	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
38	
39	## Usage
40	
41	```
42	vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
43	vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
44	vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
45	vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
46	vc-x1 validate-desc [OPTS]                 # Validate commit descriptions
47	vc-x1 fix-desc [OPTS]                     # Fix commit descriptions (dry-run default)
48	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]       # Squash working copy into target
49	vc-x1 --version                            # Print version
50	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
51	```
52	
53	**The `..` notation:** dots on the revision show which direction to
54	list commits:
55	
56	- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
57	- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
58	- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
59	
60	COMMITS is the total number of commits to show (x is always included).
61	Without dots, `x` with a count defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
62	For `..x..`, the budget is split: ancestors get the extra on odd counts.
63	
64	```
65	vc-x1 list -r @              # just @ (1 commit)
66	vc-x1 list -r @.. -n 5       # @ + 4 ancestors (5 commits, @ at top)
67	vc-x1 list -r ..@ -n 3       # 2 descendants + @ (3 commits, @ at bottom)
68	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 3     # 1 descendant, @, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
69	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 4     # 1 descendant, @, 2 ancestors (4 commits)
70	```
71	
72	### Shell completion
73	
74	vc-x1 provides tab completion for all subcommands and flags using claps
75	[unstable-dynamic](https://docs.rs/clap_complete/latest/clap_complete/env/struct.CompleteEnv.html)
76	only feature. It is a "simple" implementation and does not handle completing
77	revisions but still useful. To enable it, add one of the following to your shell's startup file:
78	
79	```bash
80	# bash (~/.bashrc)
81	source <(COMPLETE=bash vc-x1)
82	
83	# zsh (~/.zshrc)
84	source <(COMPLETE=zsh vc-x1)
85	
86	# fish (~/.config/fish/config.fish)
87	source (COMPLETE=fish vc-x1 | psub)
88	```
89	
90	Completions are generated dynamically by the binary, so they stay in
91	sync with the installed version automatically.
92	
93	### Positional shorthand
94	
95	REVISION and COMMITS can be given as positional arguments, omitting
96	`-r` and `-n`:
97	
98	```
99	vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
100	vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 4 ancestors (5 commits)
101	vc-x1 list x.. 5            # same as above
102	vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 2 descendants + x (3 commits)
103	vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 1 descendant, x, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
104	vc-x1 list ..x.. 1          # just x (1 commit)
105	```
106	
107	**Defaults:** with no arguments, REVISION is `@` and COMMITS is 1
108	(just the working copy).
109	
110	Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, and `-R`/`--repo`
111	take precedence over positional arguments.
112	
113	### Multi-repo queries
114	
115	The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query
116	multiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is
117	labeled with bold `=== path ===` headers by default:
118	
119	```
120	vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag
121	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated
122	vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands
123	```
124	
125	Control the label between repos with `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`:
126	
127	```
128	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default label: === path ===
129	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"   # custom label:  --- path ---
130	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)
131	```
132	
133	Examples:
134	
135	```
136	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude
137	=== . ===
138	kwoyvposvsmv
139	
140	=== .claude ===
141	zzpksklxyrnw
142	
143	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude -L
144	kwoyvposvsmv
145	zzpksklxyrnw
146	
147	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude
148	=== . ===
149	kwoyvposvsmv
150	
151	=== .claude ===
152	zzpksklxyrnw
153	
154	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
155	kwoyvposvsmv
156	zzpksklxyrnw
157	
158	$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude
159	=== . ===
160	kwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)
161	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
162	ssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
163	
164	=== .claude ===
165	zzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)
166	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
167	slmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
168	
169	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
170	kwoyvposvsmv
171	zzpksklxyrnw
172	
173	$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -R .,.claude
174	=== . ===
175	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
176	
177	    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.
178	    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.
179	
180	    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp
181	
182	=== .claude ===
183	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
184	
185	    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.
186	
187	    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot
188	
189	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"
190	--- . ---
191	kwoyvposvsmv
192	
193	--- .claude ---
194	zzpksklxyrnw
195	```
196	
197	Most decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,
198	`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters
199	like `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes
200	won't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).
201	
202	With a single repo (or no `-R`), no label is printed — backward
203	compatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for
204	`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.
205	
206	### validate-desc
207	
208	Read-only scan of commit descriptions against the other repo. The other
209	repo is read from `.vc-config.toml` (`workspace.other-repo`) by default,
210	or overridden with `--other-repo`. Reports status per commit: `ok`
211	(valid ochid), `lost` (ochid: lost), `none` (ochid: none), `err`
212	(issues found), or `miss` (no ochid trailer).
213	
214	```
215	# Validate all ancestors of @ (reads other repo from .vc-config.toml)
216	vc-x1 validate-desc @..
217	
218	# Validate with explicit other repo
219	vc-x1 validate-desc --other-repo .claude @..
220	
221	# Validate specific range
222	vc-x1 validate-desc -r @.. -n 20
223	```
224	
225	Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
226	
227	### fix-desc
228	
229	Fix commit descriptions against the other repo. Default is dry-run —
230	use `--no-dry-run` to write changes. Reads other repo from
231	`.vc-config.toml` by default.
232	
233	```
234	# Dry-run: show what would be fixed
235	vc-x1 fix-desc @..
236	
237	# Actually fix
238	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run
239	
240	# Add missing ochid trailers by matching title
241	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --add-missing
242	
243	# Limit fixes
244	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --no-dry-run -m 3
245	
246	# Use a fallback for IDs not found in other repo
247	vc-x1 fix-desc @.. --fallback /.claude/lost
248	```
249	
250	| Flag | Description |
251	|------|-------------|
252	| `--other-repo <PATH>` | Override other repo from .vc-config.toml |
253	| `--no-dry-run` | Write fixes [default: dry-run] |
254	| `--add-missing` | Infer and add ochid for commits without one |
255	| `-m, --max-fixes <N>` | Stop fixing after N commits changed [default: all] |
256	| `--fallback <VALUE>` | Replacement for IDs not found in other repo |
257	| `--id-len <N>` | Expected changeID length [default: 12] |
258	| `--title <TEXT>` | Replace commit title at the same time |
259	
260	Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
261	
262	### finalize
263	
264	Solves the session repo coherence problem: when the bot commits `.claude`,
265	the act of committing generates more session data. `finalize` waits,
266	then squashes the trailing writes into the target commit.
267	
268	By default finalize runs in the foreground. Use `--detach` to run in
269	the background (the bot uses this so the session can end immediately).
270	
271	The bot's last action in a session:
272	
273	```
274	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark main --delay 10 --detach --push
275	```
276	
277	If there is non-written session data after a session ends (e.g.
278	finalize failed or was skipped), run it manually:
279	
280	```
281	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark main --push
282	```
283	
284	This runs in the foreground, squashes, advances the bookmark, and pushes.
285	
286	See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
287	for full option reference and design details.
288	
289	### Testing finalize
290	
291	Always test against a throwaway jj repo, never the live workspace.
292	Use `mktemp -d` for unique directories so results can't be confused
293	with a previous run:
294	
295	```bash
296	# Create and init a temp repo
297	dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
298	(cd "$dir" && jj git init)
299	
300	# Foreground (default, blocks, logs to test dir)
301	vc-x1 finalize --repo "$dir" --bookmark main --log "$dir/finalize.log"
302	cat "$dir/finalize.log"
303	
304	# Detached (returns immediately, child logs to test dir)
305	dir2=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
306	(cd "$dir2" && jj git init)
307	vc-x1 finalize --detach --repo "$dir2" --bookmark main --log "$dir2/finalize.log"
308	cat "$dir2/finalize.log"
309	```
310	
311	The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs, covering
312	the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit, `detach`
313	spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
314	
315	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
316	
317	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
318	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
319	(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
320	and jj changeID:
321	
322	```
323	ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
324	ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
325	```
326	
327	Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
328	Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
329	within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
330	
331	For full details see:
332	- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
333	  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
334	  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
335	  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
336	
337	## jj Tips for Git Users
338	
339	If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
340	tools like `gitk --all`.
341	
342	### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
343	
344	jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
345	When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
346	jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
347	undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
348	shows the current version of each change.
349	
350	### Useful commands
351	
352	| Command | Description |
353	|---------|-------------|
354	| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
355	| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
356	| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
357	| `jj obslog -r <change-id>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
358	| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
359	
360	In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
361	the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
362

Both repos are initialized with `git` and `jj`,\nconfigured with `.vc-config.toml`, and pushed to GitHub. The session\nrepo is added as a submodule so `git clone --recursive` clones both.\n\n```\n# Create public project in current directory\nvc-x1 init my-project\n\n# Specify owner and parent directory\nvc-x1 init my-project --owner myorg --dir ~/projects\n\n# Create private repos\nvc-x1 init my-project --private\n\n# Preview without executing\nvc-x1 init my-project --dry-run\n```\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `--owner <OWNER>` | GitHub user/org [default: current `gh` user] |\n| `--dir <PATH>` | Parent directory [default: cwd] |\n| `--private` | Create private GitHub repos [default: public] |\n| `--dry-run` | Show what would be done without executing |\n\nRequires `gh` (authenticated) and `jj` to be installed.\n\n### symlink\n\nCreate or verify the Claude Code project symlink. Claude Code stores\nsession data in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/`. This command\ncreates a symlink from that location to the local `.claude` directory.\n\n```\n# Create symlink for current project (default target: .claude)\nvc-x1 symlink\n\n# Specify a different target\nvc-x1 symlink /path/to/session-dir\n\n# Replace existing symlink without prompting\nvc-x1 symlink -y\n\n# List contents after creation\nvc-x1 symlink -l\n```\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `--symlink-dir <PATH>` | Override symlink parent [default: ~/.claude/projects] |\n| `-l, --list` | List contents of symlinked directory after creation |\n| `-y, --yes` | Replace existing symlink without prompting |\n\n### finalize\n\nSolves the session repo coherence problem"

Actually — let me re-read the flow. The session repo commits first with an ochid pointing to the code repo. But the code repo doesn't have jj yet, so there's no changeID. We could either:
1. Commit the session repo without an ochid to the code repo, then fix it later
2. Init jj on the code repo temporarily just to get the changeID, then remove it before submodule work

Actually, the simplest fix: init jj on the code repo temporarily to get the changeID, then uninit it before submodule work. But that's messy.

Better approach: commit the session repo first without ochid, do all the code repo work, init jj on both at the end, then amend the ochid into the session repo's commit.

Actually, let me think about this more carefully. The code repo flow is:
1. `git init` (no jj)
2. Write config files
3. Need session repo committed first to get its changeID for code repo ochid
4. `git add` + `git commit` the code repo (with submodule)
5. `git push`
6. `jj git init --colocate` + `jj bookmark set` at the very end

For the session repo ochid pointing to code repo — we don't have a jj changeID for the code repo at commit time. Let me just skip the ochid in the session repo's initial commit, or use the git commit hash instead. Before moving on to clone, we need to add "fix" not having the ochid in .claude repo, I think the solution is to do a update .claude description after we done the `jj git init` on the code repo and squash that into .claude repo. Do you have a better idea?

> AGENT

`jj describe` on `.claude`'s `@-` is simpler than squash — it rewrites the commit message in place. After step 9 inits jj on the code repo, we can grab the code repo's changeID and do:

```
jj describe @- -m "Initial commit\n\nochid: /<code_chid>" -R .claude
jj git push --bookmark main -R .claude  # update the remote
```

No squash needed, no extra commits. Just a message rewrite + re-push of the session repo.

> DEVELOPER

SG, but don't forget we'll need a --ignore-immutable :)

> AGENT

Right — it's already been pushed so jj considers it immutable. The sequence after step 9 would be:

```
jj describe @- --ignore-immutable -m "..." -R .claude

> AGENT

Looks like a GitHub propagation delay — the repo was just created but isn't ready for SSH push yet. The `.claude` push works because there are more steps between `gh repo create` and `jj git push`, giving GitHub time to propagate.

Simplest fix: add a short sleep (2-3 seconds) between `gh repo create` and `git push` for the code repo. Or we could retry the push with a small delay. What's your preference?

> DEVELOPER

Plausible, as I do see it on github, obviously after a lots of seconds.
How about we poll N times with every S seconds (as options) instead of blind failure?

> AGENT

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/init.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Run a command, printing it first. Returns stdout on success.\nfn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {",
  "new_string": "/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.\nfn run_retry(\n    cmd: &str,\n    args: &[&str],\n    cwd: &Path,\n    retries: u32,\n    delay_secs: u64,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut last_err = String::new();\n    for attempt in 1..=retries {\n        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {\n            Ok(out) => return Ok(out),\n            Err(e) => {\n                last_err = e.to_string();\n                if attempt < retries {\n                    eprintln!(\"  retry {attempt}/{retries} in {delay_secs}s...\");\n                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Err(format!(\"failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}\").into())\n}\n\n/// Run a command, printing it first. Returns stdout on success.\nfn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {"
}

> AGENT

Clean. Both pushes (session repo and code repo) now retry. Ready for retest.

> DEVELOPER

Actually I wonder if we should wait for the gh's to complete, maybe no difference, thoughts?

> AGENT

You're right to wonder. `gh repo create` returns when the API responds, but the repo might not be SSH-accessible yet — GitHub needs to propagate the repo to the git backend servers. So the delay isn't about `gh` completing, it's about GitHub's internal propagation.

The retry approach handles this correctly regardless — it doesn't matter *why* the push fails, it just tries again. A `gh api` poll would be checking the wrong thing (API knows about the repo immediately, but SSH access lags behind).

I think what we have is the right solution.

> DEVELOPER

SG

> AGENT

Ready for you to retest when you've cleaned up the failed repos.

> DEVELOPER

I think it'd be good to have a verbose mode so we can see the retires and "always" indicate number of retires were required on success?

  "old_string": "/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.\nfn run_retry(\n    cmd: &str,\n    args: &[&str],\n    cwd: &Path,\n    retries: u32,\n    delay_secs: u64,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut last_err = String::new();\n    for attempt in 1..=retries {\n        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {\n            Ok(out) => return Ok(out),\n            Err(e) => {\n                last_err = e.to_string();\n                if attempt < retries {\n                    eprintln!(\"  retry {attempt}/{retries} in {delay_secs}s...\");\n                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Err(format!(\"failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}\").into())",
  "new_string": "/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.\nfn run_retry(\n    cmd: &str,\n    args: &[&str],\n    cwd: &Path,\n    retries: u32,\n    delay_secs: u64,\n    verbose: bool,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut last_err = String::new();\n    for attempt in 1..=retries {\n        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {\n            Ok(out) => {\n                if attempt > 1 {\n                    eprintln!(\"  succeeded after {attempt} attempts\");\n                }\n                return Ok(out);\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                last_err = e.to_string();\n                if attempt < retries {\n                    if verbose {\n                        eprintln!(\"  attempt {attempt}/{retries} failed: {last_err}\");\n                    }\n                    eprintln!(\"  retrying in {delay_secs}s...\");\n                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Err(format!(\"failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}\").into())"
}

> AGENT

Two related issues — both caused by `jj describe --ignore-immutable` creating a second commit with the same changeID (divergent). The validate-desc failure is because the .claude repo is in a confused state.

The fix: do the ochid fixup with pure git (`git commit --amend` + `git push --force`) *before* `jj git init --colocate` on the session dir. That way jj only ever sees one version.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/init.rs",
  "old_string": "    // Step 9: Initialize jj on both repos now that submodule is set up\n    eprintln!(\"\\nInitializing jj...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    // Re-init jj in the submodule (git submodule add cloned fresh)\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 10: Fix session repo ochid — now that code repo has jj, get its changeID\n    eprintln!(\"\\nFixing session repo ochid...\");\n    let code_chid_output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\n            \"log\",\n            \"-r\",\n            \"@-\",\n            \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\",\n            \"change_id\",\n            \"--limit\",\n            \"1\",\n        ])\n        .current_dir(&project_dir)\n        .output()?;\n    let code_chid_full = String::from_utf8_lossy(&code_chid_output.stdout)\n        .trim()\n        .to_string();\n    let code_chid = &code_chid_full[..code_chid_full.len().min(12)];\n    let fixed_session_body = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"describe\",\n            \"@-\",\n            \"--ignore-immutable\",\n            \"-m\",\n            &fixed_session_body,\n        ],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    // Track the remote bookmark (submodule add re-cloned, so jj doesn't know about it)\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &session_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"push\", \"--bookmark\", \"main\"], &session_dir)?;",
  "new_string": "    // Step 9: Init jj on code repo and get its changeID\n    eprintln!(\"\\nInitializing jj...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 10: Fix session repo ochid using pure git (before jj init to avoid divergence)\n    eprintln!(\"\\nFixing session repo ochid...\");\n    let code_chid_output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\n            \"log\",\n            \"-r\",\n            \"@-\",\n            \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\",\n            \"change_id\",\n            \"--limit\",\n            \"1\",\n        ])\n        .current_dir(&project_dir)\n        .output()?;\n    let code_chid_full = String::from_utf8_lossy(&code_chid_output.stdout)\n        .trim()\n        .to_string();\n    let code_chid = &code_chid_full[..code_chid_full.len().min(12)];\n    let fixed_session_body = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n    // Use git commit --amend before jj init so jj only ever sees one version\n    run(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"commit\", \"--amend\", \"-m\", &fixed_session_body],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    run(\"git\", &[\"push\", \"--force\", \"origin\", \"main\"], &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Now init jj on the session repo (clean state, no divergence)\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &session_dir)?;"
